1	\section{Consistency Check: $\Kstar$-Guided Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	$\Kempirical = 2$ makes a falsifiable prediction: binary curation should suffice, and graduated performance should converge toward binary as the decay steepness increases.
5	We test this as a consistency check on the boundary measurement, not as an independent contribution.
6	We compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): (i)~\textbf{no-filter}, (ii)~\textbf{binary-ours} ($w = 1 - P(\text{synthetic})$ from 15D LightGBM), (iii)~\textbf{binary-Drayson}~\citep{drayson2025}, (iv)~\textbf{graduated} ($w = \exp(-\alpha \cdot \hat{d})$, $\alpha \in \{0.1,\ldots,0.9\}$), (v)~\textbf{surprisal} (retain above median), and (vi)~\textbf{DSIR}~\citep{xie2023dsir}.
7	We fine-tune Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct via LoRA (rank~8, $\alpha_{\text{LoRA}}{=}16$) for 3 epochs on MMLU and HellaSwag across 5 seeds (51 total runs; this 100\% synthetic setting is an upper bound~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).
8

10	\centering
11	\small
12	\caption{Downstream task performance (MMLU, HellaSwag) under six data-filtering strategies. Binary-ours uses the 15D LightGBM depth classifier; Graduated applies exponentially decaying weights $w = \exp(-\alpha \hat{d})$. Mean $\pm$ std over 5 seeds. Full $\alpha$ sweep in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.}

14	\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}
15	\end{table}

18	These effects are measured in a 100\% synthetic upper-bound setting; real-world contamination ratios are typically lower, and effect sizes would scale accordingly.

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.
5	$\Kempirical \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ depends on source domain and model characteristics: general web text yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ in 6/10 configurations (three of six families contain $\Kempirical{\geq}2$ configurations, with a scale-dependent split in Pythia; 1B--8B; $p \leq 0.002$), with $\Kempirical{=}3$ for structured academic text.
6	This one-wash laundering effect means that a single generation pass renders web text indistinguishable from deeper recursive content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.
7	For practitioners, binary filtering (depth 0 vs.\ $\geq$1) captures the only reliable discrimination signal; graduated weighting beyond $\Kempirical$ adds noise.
8	$\Kempirical$ is feature-set dependent (15D provides a conservative lower bound; neural probes extend it by one level for some architectures) and established for 1B--8B models; scaling beyond 8B, additional structured domains, and retrain-chain $\Kstar$ remain open.
9

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
11	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$.
12	A single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.
13	Converging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\ OBD; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
14	
15	\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}
16	For web text, 2v3 $\auc < \theta$ across all model configurations, so depth assignments for $d \geq 2$ approximate random labels; graduated weighting injects noise, explaining convergence toward binary at high $\alpha$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
17	DSIR fails differently: bigram hashing captures domain shift but not depth-correlated signatures.
18	
19	\paragraph{Representation dependence.}
20	$\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).
21	The 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.
22	Feature rankings are scorer-independent ($\rho > 0.90$); $\Kempirical$ agreement is scorer-independent for strong-signal configurations (e.g., Qwen-rewrite retains $\Kempirical{=}2$ under all scorers) but scorer-sensitive at marginal boundaries (
…[truncated 3646 chars]

```json
  "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.\n$\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ across all ten configurations (six architecture families, 1B--8B) at $\\theta{=}0.60$; for general web text, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale, while structured academic text (arXiv) reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent architectures.\nAmong the six nominal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ web text configurations, four retain significance under Bonferroni correction; the two marginal cases reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.\nThis one-wash laundering effect means that a single generation pass renders web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper recursive content, concentrating all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary.\n\nThe practical consequence is direct: for web text, binary filtering (depth 0 vs.\\ $\\geq$1) captures the only reliable discrimination signal, and graduated weighting beyond $\\Kempirical$ injects noise from near-random depth assignments.\nThe consistency check confirms this prediction, with binary curation most effectively minimizing MMLU degradation among six strategies tested ($-$0.77\\,pp vs.\\ $-$1.53\\,pp for no-filter; Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\nFor structured domains where $\\Kempirical{=}3$, graduated strategies can exploit one additional depth level, so corpus curators should calibrate their filtering approach to the source domain rather than assuming a universal boundary.\n\nThe 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining: continuation-mode classifiers retain 80--92\\% accuracy across model families, and feature rankings remain stable ($\\rho > 0.90$) under alternative scorers.\nThis scorer independence makes the framework applicable to corpora generated by unknown or heterogeneous models, the common scenario in web-scale data curation where the generating model is rarely identified.\n$\\Kempirical$ converts a continuous AUC decay surface into a discrete operating boundary, providing a principled basis for curation design that replaces ad hoc depth thresholds with a measurement grounded in the Data Processing Inequality.\n\nSeveral directions remain open.\nAs model scale increases, the 0v1 signal attenuates (JSD $0.077$ at 8B vs.\\ $0.15$--$0.27$ at $\\leq$1.5B), suggesting that models beyond 13B may increasingly yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$, collapsing depth estimation to the binary boundary already dominant at current scales; tracking this trend is important for the continued viability of depth estimation.\n$\\Kempirical$ is feature-set dependent: the 15D features provide a conservative lower bound, while neural probes extend it by one level for some architectures but sacrifice cross-model consistency.\nExtending the measurement to models beyond 8B, additional structured domains (legal, medical), retrain-chain generation protocols, and scorer-ensemble strategies for improved robustness at marginal boundaries are natural next steps.\n"
}
```

```json
{
```
```json
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/conclusion.tex",
```
```json
  "old_string": "Several directions remain open.\nAs model scale increases, the 0v1 signal attenuates (JSD $0.077$ at 8B vs.\\ $0.15$--$0.27$ at $\\leq$1.5B), suggesting that models beyond 13B may increasingly yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$, collapsing depth estimation to the binary boundary already dominant at current scales; tracking this trend is important for the continued viability of depth estimation.\n$\\Kempirical$ is feature-set dependent: the 15D features provide a conservative lower bound, while neural probes extend it by one level for some architectures but sacrifice cross-model consistency.\nExtending the measurement to models beyond 8B, additional structured domains (legal, medical), retrain-chain generation protocols, and scorer-ensemble strategies for improved robustness at marginal boundaries are natural next steps.\nAs recursive synthetic content continues to accumulate in web corpora, $\\Kempirical$ offers a quantitative basis for deciding how much depth resolution a curation pipeline can extract, anchoring filtering decisions in measurement rather than assumption.",
  "new_string": "Several directions remain open.\nAs model scale increases, the 0v1 signal attenuates (JSD $0.077$ at 8B vs.\\ $0.15$--$0.27$ at $\\leq$1.5B), suggesting that models beyond 13B may increasingly yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$, collapsing depth estimation to the binary boundary already dominant at current scales; tracking this trend is important for the continued viability of depth estimation.\n$\\Kempirical$ is feature-set dependent: the 15D features provide a conservative lower bound, while neural probes extend it by one level for some architectures but sacrifice cross-model consistency.\nThe retrain-chain setting, where each generation also modifies model parameters, yields substantially higher $\\Kempirical$ ($\\geq$4 for Pythia-1.4B LoRA); bridging the prompt-chain and retrain-chain boundaries would connect our text-level measurement to the model collapse literature.\nExtending the measurement to models beyond 8B, additional structured domains (legal, medical text, where stylistic constraints may amplify depth signal as observed for arXiv), and scorer-ensemble strategies for improved robustness at marginal boundaries are natural next steps.\nAs recursive synthetic content continues to accumulate in web corpora, $\\Kempirical$ offers a quantitative basis for deciding how much depth resolution a curation pipeline can extract, anchoring filtering decisions in measurement rather than assumption."
}
```

</details>

[Director 指令] [Patch] 基于 Reviewer 叙事评估，4 个小修改：

**1. Abstract + Conclusion 叙事顺序调整**
当前："K*≤3 across all ten configurations" 出现在 "K*≤2 for web text" 之前。应反过来——"K*≤2 web text" 是更锐利的 finding，放前面；"≤3 universally" 作为附带泛化放后面。Abstract 和 Conclusion 都检查调整。

**2. Discussion 加 Bonferroni 同步句**
Discussion "boundary cases" 或 "What K* reveals" 段当前没提 Bonferroni dual report，但 Abstract 和 Experiments 都提了。加一句类似："Under Bonferroni correction (99.5% CI for 10 simultaneous tests), four of the six nominal K*=2 configurations retain significance, while two boundary cases (Qwen-cont and LLaMA-8B at AUC ≈ 0.598) fall marginally below θ." 使三处叙事同步。

**3. Discussion "What K* reveals" 段补强实用价值 [重要]**
Reviewer 指出攻击面：reviewer 会问 "如果 realistic contamination 下 filtering 没区别，为什么需要 K*？"。在 "What K* reveals" 段加 2-3 句直接回应：
- K* 的价值不在改变 filtering 策略（binary 已足够），而在于：(1) 为 binary sufficiency 提供 empirical justification 而非 heuristic assumption；(2) 识别 domain-specific exceptions（arXiv K*=3 允许 graduated filtering）；(3) 作为 monitoring metric 追踪模型/数据演变趋势
- 写成自然段落融入 Discussion，不要写成编号列表

**4. Cross-model transfer 叙事微调**
Reviewer 指出 cross-model transfer (80-100% retention) 被 K* characterization 盖住了。在 Abstract 的 contribution (a) 或 Discussion 中，稍微突出 "model-agnostic depth estimation without retraining" 的实用意义。不需要大改，加半句即可。

写作规则：无破折号、无 AI 风格。编译后确认 8 页主体不溢出。

33	% === Abstract ===
34	\begin{abstract}
35	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
36	We measure a lower bound on the generational discrimination boundary, $\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable), using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
37	Under prompt-chain generation across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--8B), 3 source domains, and 4 decoding strategies, $\Kempirical \leq 3$ across all ten configurations (six families, 1B--8B) at $\theta{=}0.60$; for web text (C4, Wikipedia), $\Kempirical \leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale, with structured academic text (arXiv) reaching $\Kempirical{=}3$ across two architectures.
38	Among the six nominal $\Kempirical{=}2$ configurations, four retain significance under Bonferroni correction; the two marginal cases reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.
39	A single generation pass suffices to render general web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \emph{one-wash laundering} effect.
40	Model-free statistical features enable 80--100\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$).
41	As a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\Kempirical$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text when contamination causes detectable quality loss.

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.
5	$\Kempirical \leq 3$ across all ten configurations (six architecture families, 1B--8B) at $\theta{=}0.60$; for general web text, $\Kempirical \leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale, while structured academic text (arXiv) reaches $\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent architectures.
6	Among the six nominal $\Kempirical{=}2$ web text configurations, four retain significance under Bonferroni correction; the two marginal cases reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.
7	This one-wash laundering effect means that a single generation pass renders web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper recursive content, concentrating all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary.
8	
9	The practical consequence is direct: for web text, binary filtering (depth 0 vs.\ $\geq$1) captures the only reliable discrimination signal, and graduated weighting beyond $\Kempirical$ injects noise from near-random depth assignments.
10	The consistency check confirms this prediction, with binary curation most effectively minimizing MMLU degradation among six strategies tested ($-$0.77\,pp vs.\ $-$1.53\,pp for no-filter; Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
11	For structured domains where $\Kempirical{=}3$, graduated strategies can exploit one additional depth level, so corpus curators should calibrate their filtering approach to the source domain rather than assuming a universal boundary.
12	
13	The 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining: continuation-mode classifiers retain 80--92\% accuracy across model families, and scorer-independent feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$) make the framework applicable to corpora generated by unknown or heterogeneous models.

_source line 11217 · 2026-05-22T11:40:18.405885+00:00_

35	\paragraph{Boundary cases and scale effect.}
36	$\Kempirical{=}1$ configurations cluster at 1v2 $\auc$ 0.508--0.598, well separated from the $\Kempirical{=}2$ range (0.611--0.696); $\Kempirical{=}1$ is not a classifier failure but a text property, as these models produce first-generation output so human-like that statistical features cannot distinguish depth~1 from depth~2.
37	That $\Kempirical \in \{1, 2\}$ for all web text configurations is the core empirical result; larger models reduce 0v1 JSD (0.077 at 8B vs.\ 0.15--0.27 at $\leq$1.5B), suggesting 13B+ may increasingly yield $\Kempirical{=}1$.
38	
39	\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}
40	Greedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-6.9B yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ under greedy but $\Kempirical{=}1$ under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$), with the borderline 1v2 score (2 of 5 folds above $\theta$) illustrating that the boundary is continuous rather than a cliff (Table~\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}).
41	Practitioners should calibrate $\theta$ to their application-specific tolerance (Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).
42	
43	\paragraph{What $\Kempirical$ reveals.}
44	As a measurement tool, $\Kempirical$ provides explanatory power beyond a point estimate: it establishes why binary detection suffices for web text (depth${>}1$ is invisible to statistical features), identifies domains where finer strategies are viable (arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ vs.\ web $\Kempirical{=}2$), and quantifies how scale, scorer, and threshold jointly shape the boundary.
45	The OBD framework provides a reproducible, low-cost depth estimation protocol requiring only corpus-level statistical features, unlike detection methods that need API access or model weights.
46	Future work may explore scorer-ensemble strategies to improve cross-scorer robustness at marginal boundaries.
47

24	Our contributions:
25	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
26	  \item \textbf{$\Kempirical$ as a measurement tool} across 10 model configurations (6 families, 1B--8B) and 4 decoding strategies ($p \leq 0.002$; Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}):
27	  $\Kempirical \leq 3$ universally; for web text, $\Kempirical \leq 2$ regardless of family or scale.
28	  $\Kempirical$ establishes binary sufficiency for web text by measurement (not assumption), reveals domain-dependent variation (arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$), and quantifies boundary sensitivity to threshold, scorer, and model scale.
29	  Both supervised OBD and zero-shot LLMs fail beyond $\Kempirical$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$; Section~\ref{sec:why_kstar}).
30	  \item \textbf{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$}: general web text (C4, Wikipedia) yields $\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\Kempirical{=}3$, consistent across model families (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}).
31	  \item \textbf{Model-free cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--100\% accuracy retention with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$); $\Kempirical$ agreement holds across scorers for strong-signal configurations (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
32	\end{enumerate}
33	
34	\noindent As a consistency check, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering for web-text filtering: binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941$ vs.\ base $0.6018$; Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).

```json
  "new_string": "That $\\Kempirical \\in \\{1, 2\\}$ for all web text configurations is the core empirical result; larger models reduce 0v1 JSD (0.077 at 8B vs.\\ 0.15--0.27 at $\\leq$1.5B), suggesting 13B+ may increasingly yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$.\nUnder Bonferroni correction for 10 simultaneous comparisons (99.5\\% CIs), four of six nominal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ configurations retain significance; the two most marginal cases (Qwen-continuation and LLaMA-8B, 1v2 $\\auc \\approx 0.61$) fall below the widened interval but retain point estimates and uncorrected CIs above $\\theta$.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}"
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "The OBD framework provides a reproducible, low-cost depth estimation protocol requiring only corpus-level statistical features, unlike detection methods that need API access or model weights.\nFuture work may explore scorer-ensemble strategies to improve cross-scorer robustness at marginal boundaries.",
  "new_string": "The OBD framework provides a reproducible, low-cost depth estimation protocol requiring only corpus-level statistical features, unlike detection methods that need API access or model weights.\nEven when the practical outcome remains binary filtering, $\\Kempirical$ contributes by grounding this choice in measurement rather than heuristic assumption: practitioners no longer need to guess whether finer depth resolution is exploitable.\nIt also identifies exceptions where graduated curation is viable (arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$) and provides a monitoring metric for tracking how the discrimination boundary shifts as model scale increases and corpus composition evolves.\nFuture work may explore scorer-ensemble strategies to improve cross-scorer robustness at marginal boundaries."
}
```

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	This gap cuts across three communities: binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}); membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question; the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool; and data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.
11	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
12	
13	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$.
14	We empirically measure a lower bound $\Kempirical$ on the discrimination boundary (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) using 15 statistica
…[truncated 2187 chars]

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
5	\label{sec:formulation}
6	
7	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
8	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
9	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: mutual information with the seed text degrades monotonically.
10	Together with Markov chain convergence, this provides heuristic motivation for expecting $\auc_{k-1,k}$ to decrease with $k$, though AUC and mutual information are not formally equivalent; the actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.
11	
12	We define the operational lower bound:
13	\begin{equation}
14	  \Kempirical = \max\!\bigl\{k \!\geq\! 1 : \auc_{j-1,j} > \theta,\;\forall\, j \!\leq\! k\bigr\},
15	  \label{eq:kstar}
16	\end{equation}
17	which requires all pairs up to depth $k$ to exceed the threshold (the $\forall j$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\auc_{k-1,k}$ decreases monotonically above $\theta$ across all tested configurations; Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).
18	The theoretical upper bound $\Ktheory$ (the true maximum discriminable depth given optimal features) is not directly estimated in this work.
19	Since any specific classifier on a feature subset achieves AUC at most equal to the Bayes-optimal classifier on all features, $\Kempirical \leq \Ktheory$ follows directly.
20	The DPI separately motivates the empirical observation that $\auc_{k-1,k}$ decreases with $k$, though AUC is not a monotone function of MI (as noted above).
21	Here $\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point motivated by Cohen's small-effect-size convention ($h \approx 0.20$\footnote{$h = 2\arcsin\!\sqrt{0.60} - 2\arcsin\!\sqrt{0.50} \approx 0.2014$, a ``small'' effect size by Cohen's (1988) conven
…[truncated 1314 chars]

Based on the search results, I found the paper you're looking for. Here's information about it:

## "Unveiling Attractor Cycles in Large Language Models: A Dynamical Systems View of Successive Paraphrasing"

**Authors:** Zhilin Wang, Yafu Li, Jianhao Yan, Yu Cheng, and Yue Zhang

**Publication:** Published in 2025 at the Association for Computational Linguistics (ACL), Volume 1: Long Papers

**Key Findings:**

The study reveals that successive paraphrasing in LLMs converges to stable periodic states, such as 2-period attractor cycles, limiting linguistic diversity, rather than exploring a diverse range of paraphrases as expected. This phenomenon is attributed to the self-reinforcing nature of LLMs, as they iteratively favour and amplify certain textual forms over others.

**Main Contribution:** These findings underscore inherent constraints in LLM generative capability, while offering a novel dynamical systems perspective for studying their expressive potential.

The paper uses dynamical systems theory to analyze how LLMs behave when repeatedly paraphrasing tex

Based on the search results, I found the paper you're looking for:

"A Theoretical Perspective: How to Prevent Model Collapse in Self-consuming Training Loops" by Shi Fu and 4 other authors is available on arXiv as preprint arXiv:2502.18865, 2025.

**Key findings from the paper:**

High-quality data is essential for training large generative models, yet the vast reservoir of real data available online has become nearly depleted. Consequently, models increasingly generate their own data for further training, forming Self-consuming Training Loops (STLs).

68	%% ====================================================================
69	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}
70	\label{sec:transfer}
71	
72	\begin{figure}[t]
73	\centering
74	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf}
75	\caption{Cross-model transfer heatmap. Each cell shows 6-class ordinal accuracy when trained on the row model and tested on the column model, divided by in-domain accuracy (retention rate). Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo: 90--92\%; Qwen-rewrite$\to$continuation: 61--63\%.}
76	\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}
77	\end{figure}
78	
79	Continuation-mode classifiers transfer well across generators (Figure~\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap}; full matrix in Table~\ref{tab:s1_transfer}): Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo retains 90--92\%, Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$Pythia 84--85\%, Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$OLMo 80--85\% (0v1 cross-model $\auc > 0.96$ for all pairs).
80	Qwen-rewrite classifiers transfer poorly (61--63\%), confirming reliance on mode-specific artifacts rather than generalizable depth features.
81	
82	\paragraph{Cross-scorer validation.}
83	Replacing the self-scorer with Pythia-1.4B or OLMo-1B preserves feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$) but attenuates absolute discriminability at the boundary (Table~\ref{tab:crossscorer}).
84	Feature ranking independence is a strong result: the same features dominate under all scorers.
85	$\Kempirical$ agreement, however, is signal-strength dependent: Qwen-rewrite (strong signal, 1v2 $\auc = 0.696$) retains $\Kempirical{=}2$ under all scorers, but Qwen-continuation (marginal, 1v2 $\auc = 0.611$, only 0.011 above $\theta$) drops to $\Kempirical{=}1$ (Pythia: $0.5923$; OLMo: $0.5858$).
86	A three-way ANOVA (Generator $\times$ Scorer $\times$ Depth-Pair; Table~\ref{tab:anova_crossscorer}) confirms a statistically significant but modest Scorer main effect ($\eta^2_p{=}.166$, $p < .001$) and a significant Generator$\times$Scorer interaction ($\eta^2_p{=}.146$), indicating that absolute AUC values are s

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.
5	For general web text, $\Kempirical \leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale (six architecture families, 1B--8B, $\theta{=}0.60$); across all ten configurations, $\Kempirical \leq 3$, with structured academic text (arXiv) reaching $\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent architectures.
6	Among the six nominal $\Kempirical{=}2$ web text configurations, four retain significance under Bonferroni correction; the two marginal cases reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.
7	This one-wash laundering effect means that a single generation pass renders web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper recursive content, concentrating all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary.
8	
9	The practical consequence is direct: for web text, binary filtering (depth 0 vs.\ $\geq$1) captures the only reliable discrimination signal, and graduated weighting beyond $\Kempirical$ injects noise from near-random depth assignments.
10	The consistency check confirms this prediction, with binary curation most effectively minimizing MMLU degradation among six strategies tested ($-$0.77\,pp vs.\ $-$1.53\,pp for no-filter; Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
11	For structured domains where $\Kempirical{=}3$, graduated strategies can exploit one additional depth level, so corpus curators should calibrate their filtering approach to the source domain rather than assuming a universal boundary.
12	
13	The 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining: continuation-mode classifiers retain 80--92\% accuracy across model families, and scorer-independent feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$) make the framework applicable to corpora generated by unknown or heterogeneous models.
14	$\Kempirical$ converts a continuous AUC decay surface into a discrete operating boundary, repla

```json
  "old_string": "We measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale (six architecture families, 1B--8B, $\\theta{=}0.60$); across all ten configurations, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$, with structured academic text (arXiv) reaching $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent architectures.\nAmong the six nominal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ web text configurations, four retain significance under Bonferroni correction; the two marginal cases reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.\nThis one-wash laundering effect means that a single generation pass renders web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper recursive content, concentrating all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary.\n\nThe practical consequence is direct: for web text, binary filtering (depth 0 vs.\\ $\\geq$1) captures the only reliable discrimination signal, and graduated weighting beyond $\\Kempirical$ injects noise from near-random depth assignments.\nThe consistency check confirms this prediction, with binary curation most effectively minimizing MMLU degradation among six strategies tested ($-$0.77\\,pp vs.\\ $-$1.53\\,pp for no-filter; Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\nFor structured domains where $\\Kempirical{=}3$, graduated strategies can exploit one additional depth level, so corpus curators should calibrate their filtering approach to the source domain rather than assuming a universal boundary.\n\nThe 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining: continuation-mode classifiers retain 80--92\\% accuracy across model families, and scorer-independent feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$) make the framework applicable to corpora generated by unknown or heterogeneous models.\n$\\Kempirical$ converts a continuous AUC decay surface into a discrete operating boundary, replacing ad hoc depth thresholds with a measurement grounded in the Data Processing Inequality.\nSeveral directions remain open: tracking $\\Kempirical$ as model scale surpasses 13B (where 0v1 JSD attenuation suggests $\\Kempirical{=}1$ may become dominant), bridging the prompt-chain and retrain-chain boundaries to connect text-level measurement with the model collapse literature, and extending to additional structured domains and scorer-ensemble strategies.",
  "new_string": "We measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale (six architecture families, 1B--8B, $\\theta{=}0.60$); across all ten configurations, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$, with structured academic text (arXiv) reaching $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent architectures.\nAmong the six nominal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ web text configurations, four retain significance under Bonferroni correction; the two marginal cases reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.\nThis one-wash laundering effect concentrates all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary: binary filtering captures the only reliable discrimination signal, and graduated weighting beyond $\\Kempirical$ injects noise from near-random depth assignments.\nThe consistency check confirms this prediction, with binary curation most effectively minimizing MMLU degradation ($-$0.77\\,pp vs.\\ $-$1.53\\,pp for no-filter).\nFor structured domains where $\\Kempirical{=}3$, graduated strategies can exploit one additional depth level, so corpus curators should calibrate filtering to the source domain rather than assuming a universal boundary.\n\nThe 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining (80--92\\% accuracy retention, $\\rho > 0.90$ scorer-independent rankings), and $\\Kempirical$ converts a continuous AUC decay surface into a discrete operating boundary grounded in the Data Processing Inequality.\nOpen directions include tracking $\\Kempirical$ beyond 13B scale, bridging the prompt-chain and retrain-chain boundaries, and extending to additional structured domains."
```

_source line 11331 · 2026-05-22T14:43:17.002443+00:00_

43	
44	\paragraph{What $\Kempirical$ reveals.}
45	As a measurement tool, $\Kempirical$ provides explanatory power beyond a point estimate: it establishes why binary detection suffices for web text (depth${>}1$ is invisible to statistical features), identifies domains where finer strategies are viable (arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ vs.\ web $\Kempirical{=}2$), and quantifies how scale, scorer, and threshold jointly shape the boundary.
46	The OBD framework provides a reproducible, low-cost depth estimation protocol requiring only corpus-level statistical features, unlike detection methods that need API access or model weights.
47	Even when the practical outcome remains binary filtering, $\Kempirical$ contributes by grounding this choice in measurement rather than heuristic assumption: practitioners no longer need to guess whether finer depth resolution is exploitable.
48	It also identifies exceptions where graduated curation is viable (arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$) and provides a monitoring metric for tracking how the discrimination boundary shifts as model scale increases and corpus composition evolves.
49	Future work may explore scorer-ensemble strategies to improve cross-scorer robustness at marginal boundaries.
50

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate generational depth estimation on ten model configurations spanning six architecture families:
5	Pythia-1.4B, Pythia-6.9B, OLMo-1B, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base), Qwen2.5-7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B, and Gemma-2-2B, all in continuation mode except where noted.
6	All experiments use the OBD-LightGBM classifier with the 15-dimensional feature set (Section~\ref{sec:method}), with 5{,}000 texts per depth level (generations 0--5).

1	\section{Consistency Check: $\Kstar$-Guided Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	$\Kempirical = 2$ makes a falsifiable prediction: binary curation should suffice, and graduated performance should converge toward binary as the decay steepness increases.
5	We test this as a consistency check on the boundary measurement, not as an independent contribution.
6	We compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): (i)~\textbf{no-filter}, (ii)~\textbf{binary-ours} ($w = 1 - P(\text{synthetic})$ from 15D LightGBM), (iii)~\textbf{binary-Drayson}~\citep{drayson2025}, (iv)~\textbf{graduated} ($w = \exp(-\alpha \cdot \hat{d})$, $\alpha \in \{0.1,\ldots,0.9\}$), (v)~\textbf{surprisal} (retain above median), and (vi)~\textbf{DSIR}~\citep{xie2023dsir}.
7	We fine-tune Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct via LoRA (rank~8, $\alpha_{\text{LoRA}}{=}16$) for 3 epochs on MMLU and HellaSwag across 5 seeds (51 total runs; this 100\% synthetic setting is an upper bound~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).
8	
9	\begin{table}[t]
10	\centering
11	\small
12	\caption{Downstream task performance (MMLU, HellaSwag) under six data-filtering strategies. Binary-ours uses the 15D LightGBM depth classifier; Graduated applies exponentially decaying weights $w = \exp(-\alpha \hat{d})$. Mean $\pm$ std over 5 seeds. Full $\alpha$ sweep in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.}
13	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
14	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}
15	\end{table}
16	
17	Binary-ours minimizes MMLU degradation most effectively (Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}), reducing the gap from $-$1.53\,pp (no-filter) to $-$0.77\,pp (${\sim}$50\% reduction; $p_{\text{corr}} < 0.05$ vs.\ all alternatives).
18	These effects are measured in a 100\% synthetic upper-bound setting; real-world contamination ratios are typically lower, and effect sizes would scale accordingly.

```json
  "old_string": "%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Binary Detector Confidence}\n\\label{sec:binary_confidence}",
  "new_string": "%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Robustness: Segment Length and Seed Variation}\n\\label{sec:robustness}\n\n\\paragraph{Segment length.}\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ is confirmed for segment lengths of 128, 256, and 512 tokens (Qwen2.5-1.5B base, continuation mode; Table~\\ref{tab:s11_length_ablation}).\nThe 1v2 AUC remains above $\\theta$ for 128 tokens (0.622) and 256 tokens (0.628); at 512 tokens the point estimate (0.607) exceeds $\\theta$ but the bootstrap CI lower bound falls marginally below ($[0.598, 0.617]$), indicating that the boundary is robust to input length but becomes marginal at longer segments where increased token counts partially compensate for per-generation information loss.\nThe 2v3 AUC remains below $\\theta$ at all lengths ($\\leq 0.572$), confirming that the boundary between discriminable and indiscriminable depths is length-invariant.\n\n\\paragraph{Seed variation.}\nRandom seeds govern both corpus sampling and generation quality.\nWe re-run three configurations under an alternative seed (seed $= 123$ vs.\\ primary seed $= 42$; Table~\\ref{tab:seed_robustness}).\n$\\Kempirical$ is stable in 2/3 cases: Pythia-1.4B on Wikipedia yields $\\Kempirical{=}1$ under both seeds (1v2 $\\Delta = -0.8$\\,pp), and Qwen-1.5B on C4 yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\\Delta = +9.0$\\,pp, both seeds above $\\theta$).\nQwen-1.5B on Wikipedia shifts from $\\Kempirical{=}2$ to $\\Kempirical{=}3$ under the alternative seed, but the 2v3 AUC ($0.607$) is marginal: one of five folds falls below $\\theta$.\nThe ${\\pm}9$\\,pp absolute AUC variation across seeds strengthens the methodological case for threshold-based $\\Kempirical$ over raw AUC comparisons: the qualitative boundary is robust even when point estimates shift.\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Binary Detector Confidence}\n\\label{sec:binary_confidence}"
}
```

43	
44	\paragraph{What $\Kempirical$ reveals.}
45	As a measurement tool, $\Kempirical$ provides explanatory power beyond a point estimate: it establishes why binary detection suffices for web text (depth${>}1$ is invisible to statistical features), identifies domains where finer strategies are viable (arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ vs.\ web $\Kempirical{=}2$), and quantifies how scale, scorer, and threshold jointly shape the boundary.
46	The OBD framework provides a reproducible, low-cost depth estimation protocol requiring only corpus-level statistical features, unlike detection methods that need API access or model weights.
47	Even when the practical outcome remains binary filtering, $\Kempirical$ contributes by grounding this choice in measurement rather than heuristic assumption: practitioners no longer need to guess whether finer depth resolution is exploitable.
48	It also identifies exceptions where graduated curation is viable (arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$) and provides a monitoring metric as model scale increases and corpus composition evolves.
49

_source line 11397 · 2026-05-22T15:26:15.018208+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.
5	For general web text, $\Kempirical \leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale (six architecture families, 1B--8B, $\theta{=}0.60$); across all ten configurations, $\Kempirical \leq 3$, with structured academic text (arXiv) reaching $\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent architectures.
6	Among the six nominal $\Kempirical{=}2$ web text configurations, four retain significance under Bonferroni correction; the two marginal cases reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.
7	This one-wash laundering effect concentrates all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary: binary filtering captures the only reliable discrimination signal, and graduated weighting beyond $\Kempirical$ injects noise from near-random depth assignments.
8	The consistency check confirms this prediction, with binary curation most effectively minimizing MMLU degradation ($-$0.77\,pp vs.\ $-$1.53\,pp for no-filter).
9	For structured domains where $\Kempirical{=}3$, graduated strategies can exploit one additional depth level, so corpus curators should calibrate filtering to the source domain rather than assuming a universal boundary.
10	
11	The 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining (80--92\% accuracy retention, $\rho > 0.90$ scorer-independent rankings), and $\Kempirical$ converts a continuous AUC decay surface into a discrete operating boundary grounded in the Data Processing Inequality.
12	Open directions include tracking $\Kempirical$ beyond 13B scale and bridging the prompt-chain and retrain-chain boundaries.
13

150	%% ====================================================================
151	\subsection{Robustness: Segment Length and Seed Variation}
152	\label{sec:robustness}
153	
154	\paragraph{Segment length.}
155	$\Kempirical{=}2$ is confirmed for segment lengths of 128, 256, and 512 tokens (Qwen2.5-1.5B base, continuation mode; Table~\ref{tab:s11_length_ablation}).
156	The 1v2 AUC remains above $\theta$ for 128 tokens (0.622) and 256 tokens (0.628); at 512 tokens the point estimate (0.607) exceeds $\theta$ but the bootstrap CI lower bound falls marginally below ($[0.598, 0.617]$), indicating that the boundary is robust to input length but becomes marginal at longer segments where increased token counts partially compensate for per-generation information loss.
157	The 2v3 AUC remains below $\theta$ at all lengths ($\leq 0.572$), confirming that the boundary between discriminable and indiscriminable depths is length-invariant.
158	
159	\paragraph{Seed variation.}
160	Random seeds govern both corpus sampling and generation quality.
161	We re-run three configurations under an alternative seed (seed $= 123$ vs.\ primary seed $= 42$; Table~\ref{tab:seed_robustness}).
162	$\Kempirical$ is stable in 2/3 cases: Pythia-1.4B on Wikipedia yields $\Kempirical{=}1$ under both seeds (1v2 $\Delta = -0.8$\,pp), and Qwen-1.5B on C4 yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\Delta = +9.0$\,pp, both seeds above $\theta$).
163	Qwen-1.5B on Wikipedia shifts from $\Kempirical{=}2$ to $\Kempirical{=}3$ under the alternative seed, but the 2v3 AUC ($0.607$) is marginal: one of five folds falls below $\theta$.
164	The ${\pm}9$\,pp absolute AUC variation across seeds strengthens the methodological case for threshold-based $\Kempirical$ over raw AUC comparisons: the qualitative boundary is robust even when point estimates shift.
165	
166	%% ====================================================================
167	\subsection{Binary Detector Confidence}
168	\label{sec:binary_confidence}

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
11	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$.
12	A single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.
13	Converging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\ OBD; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
14	
15	\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}
16	For web text, 2v3 $\auc < \theta$ across all model configurations, so depth assignments for $d \geq 2$ approximate random labels; graduated weighting injects noise, explaining convergence toward binary at high $\alpha$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
17	DSIR fails differently: bigram hashing captures domain shift but not depth-correlated signatures.
18	
19	\paragraph{Representation dependence.}
20	$\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).
21	The 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.
22	Feature rankings are scorer-independent ($\rho > 0.90$); $\Kempirical$ agreement is scorer-independent for strong-signal configurations (e.g., Qwen-rewrite retains $\Kempirical{=}2$ under all scorers) but scorer-sensitive at marginal boundaries (
…[truncated 4459 chars]

```json
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) consistently yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent model families (Qwen-1.5B, Pythia-1.4B; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\nDomain-specific stylistic constraints amplify per-generation distributional shift, making contamination in structured corpora trackable to greater depth.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nOur prompt-chain protocol targets the dominant web contamination scenario: LLM-generated text is published, scraped into training corpora, and recursively re-generated by downstream models without retraining on the outputs.\nRetrain-chain experiments (Pythia-1.4B LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.9484$, 3v4 $= 0.7982$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary of $\\Kempirical{=}2$, because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts (vocabulary narrowing, repetition bias) absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ therefore isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines that filter documents without knowledge of the generator's training history.\nOur conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings, where the higher $\\Kempirical$ reflects model-level rather than text-level information loss.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) consistently yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent model families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), as domain-specific stylistic constraints amplify per-generation distributional shift.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments (Pythia-1.4B LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.9484$, 3v4 $= 0.7982$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary of $\\Kempirical{=}2$, because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings."
}
```

```json
    "old_string": "\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) consistently yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent model families (Qwen-1.5B, Pythia-1.4B; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\nDomain-specific stylistic constraints amplify per-generation distributional shift, making contamination in structured corpora trackable to greater depth.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nOur prompt-chain protocol targets the dominant web contamination scenario: LLM-generated text is published, scraped into training corpora, and recursively re-generated by downstream models without retraining on the outputs.\nRetrain-chain experiments (Pythia-1.4B LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.9484$, 3v4 $= 0.7982$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary of $\\Kempirical{=}2$, because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts (vocabulary narrowing, repetition bias) absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ therefore isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines that filter documents without knowledge of the generator's training history.\nOur conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings, where the higher $\\Kempirical$ reflects model-level rather than text-level information loss.",
    "new_string": "\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) consistently yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent model families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), as domain-specific stylistic constraints amplify per-generation distributional shift.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments (Pythia-1.4B LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.9484$, 3v4 $= 0.7982$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary of $\\Kempirical{=}2$, because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings."
  }
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "The 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining: continuation-mode classifiers retain 80--92\\% accuracy across model families, and scorer-independent feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$) make the framework applicable to corpora generated by unknown or heterogeneous models.\n$\\Kempirical$ converts a continuous AUC decay surface into a discrete operating boundary, replacing ad hoc depth thresholds with a measurement grounded in the Data Processing Inequality.\nSeveral directions remain open: tracking $\\Kempirical$ as model scale surpasses 13B (where 0v1 JSD attenuation suggests $\\Kempirical{=}1$ may become dominant), bridging the prompt-chain and retrain-chain boundaries to connect text-level measurement with the model collapse literature, and extending to additional structured domains and scorer-ensemble strategies.",
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/conclusion.tex",
```
```json
  "old_string": "We measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale (six architecture families, 1B--8B, $\\theta{=}0.60$); across all ten configurations, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$, with structured academic text (arXiv) reaching $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent architectures.\nAmong the six nominal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ web text configurations, four retain significance under Bonferroni correction; the two marginal cases reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.\nThis one-wash laundering effect concentrates all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary: binary filtering captures the only reliable discrimination signal, and graduated weighting beyond $\\Kempirical$ injects noise from near-random depth assignments.\nThe consistency check confirms this prediction, with binary curation most effectively minimizing MMLU degradation ($-$0.77\\,pp vs.\\ $-$1.53\\,pp for no-filter).\nFor structured domains where $\\Kempirical{=}3$, graduated strategies can exploit one additional depth level, so corpus curators should calibrate filtering to the source domain rather than assuming a universal boundary.\n\nThe 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining (80--92\\% accuracy retention, $\\rho > 0.90$ scorer-independent rankings), and $\\Kempirical$ converts a continuous AUC decay surface into a discrete operating boundary grounded in the Data Processing Inequality.\nSeveral directions remain open: tracking $\\Kempirical$ as model scale surpasses 13B, bridging the prompt-chain and retrain-chain boundaries, and extending to additional structured domains.",
  "new_string": "We measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale (six architecture families, 1B--8B, $\\theta{=}0.60$); across all ten configurations, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$, with structured academic text (arXiv) reaching $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent architectures.\nThis one-wash laundering effect concentrates all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary, and the consistency check confirms that binary curation most effectively minimizes MMLU degradation ($-$0.77\\,pp vs.\\ $-$1.53\\,pp for no-filter).\nFor structured domains where $\\Kempirical{=}3$, graduated strategies can exploit one additional depth level, so corpus curators should calibrate filtering to the source domain.\nThe 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining (80--92\\% accuracy retention, $\\rho > 0.90$), and $\\Kempirical$ converts a continuous AUC decay surface into a discrete operating boundary.\nSeveral directions remain open: tracking $\\Kempirical$ as model scale surpasses 13B, bridging the prompt-chain and retrain-chain boundaries, and extending to additional structured domains."
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
```
```json
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{What $\\Kempirical$ reveals.}\nAs a measurement tool, $\\Kempirical$ provides explanatory power beyond a point estimate: it establishes why binary detection suffices for web text (depth${>}1$ is invisible to statistical features), identifies domains where finer strategies are viable (arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ vs.\\ web $\\Kempirical{=}2$), and quantifies how scale, scorer, and threshold jointly shape the boundary.\nThe OBD framework provides a reproducible, low-cost depth estimation protocol requiring only corpus-level statistical features, unlike detection methods that need API access or model weights.\nEven when the practical outcome remains binary filtering, $\\Kempirical$ contributes by grounding this choice in measurement rather than heuristic assumption: practitioners no longer need to guess whether finer depth resolution is exploitable.\nIt also identifies exceptions where graduated curation is viable (arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$) and provides a monitoring metric for tracking how the discrimination boundary shifts as model scale increases and corpus composition evolves.\nFuture work may explore scorer-ensemble strategies to improve cross-scorer robustness at marginal boundaries.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{What $\\Kempirical$ reveals.}\nAs a measurement tool, $\\Kempirical$ establishes why binary detection suffices for web text (depth${>}1$ is invisible to statistical features), identifies domains where finer strategies are viable (arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$), and quantifies how scale, scorer, and threshold jointly shape the boundary.\nThe OBD framework provides a reproducible, low-cost depth estimation protocol requiring only corpus-level statistical features, unlike detection methods that need API access or model weights.\n$\\Kempirical$ also serves as a monitoring metric for tracking how the discrimination boundary shifts as model scale increases; future work may explore scorer-ensemble strategies to improve cross-scorer robustness at marginal boundaries."
}
```

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
11	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$.
12	A single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.
13	Converging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\ OBD; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
14	
15	\paragraph{Representation dependence.}
16	$\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).
17	The 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.
18	Feature rankings are scorer-independent ($\rho > 0.90$); $\Kempirical$ agreement is scorer-independent for strong-signal configurations (e.g., Qwen-rewrite retains $\Kempirical{=}2$ under all scorers) but scorer-sensitive at marginal boundaries (Qwen-continuation drops to $\Kempirical{=}1$; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
19	ANOVA confirms this layered pattern: Scorer explains modest variance ($\eta^2_p{=}.166$) with a significant Generator$\times$Scorer interaction ($\eta^2_p{=}.146$; Table~\ref{tab:anova_crossscorer}).
20	
21	\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$.}

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	This gap cuts across three communities: binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}); membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question; the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool; and data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.
11	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
12	
13	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$.
14	We empirically measure a lower bound $\Kempirical$ on the discrimination boundary (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) using 15 statistica
…[truncated 1793 chars]

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}
7	\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Corpus-level statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}
8	\label{fig:pipeline}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
12	\label{sec:formulation}
13	
14	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
15	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
16	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: mutual information with the seed text degrades monotonically.
17	Together with Markov chain convergence, this provides heuristic motivation for expecting $\auc_{k-1,k}$ to decrease with $k$, though AUC and mutual information are not formally equivalent; the actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.
18	
19	We define the operational lower bound:
20	\begin{equation}
21	  \Kempirical = \max\!\bigl\{k \!\geq\! 1 : \auc_{j-1,j} > \theta,\;\forall\, j \!\leq\! k\bigr\},
22	  \label{eq:kstar}
23	\end{equation}
24	which requires all pairs up to depth $k$ to exceed the threshold (the $\forall j$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\auc_{k-1,k}$ decreases monotonically above $\theta$ across all tested configurations; Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).
25	The theoretical upper bound $\Ktheory$ (the true maximum discriminable depth given optimal features) is not directly estimated in this work.
26	Since any specific classifier on a feature subset achieves AUC at most equal to the Bayes-optimal classifier on all features, $\Kempirical \leq \Ktheory$ follows directly.
27	The DPI separately motivates the empirical observation that $\auc

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model collapse and recursive degradation.}
5	Iterative training on model outputs causes tail-distribution degradation~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, dohmatob2024model, dohmatob2025strong, fu2025theoretical}, diversity contraction~\cite{guo2024curious, padmakumar2024writing, briesch2023selfconsuming}, and predictable convergence~\cite{schaeffer2025position, kazdan2025collapse, gerstgrasser2024model, wang2025unveiling}.
6	Our prompt-chain operates in the replace regime~\cite{kazdan2025collapse}, making $\Kempirical = 2$ a conservative lower bound.
7	This literature characterizes macro dynamics but provides no per-text depth estimation, the gap we address.
8	
9	\paragraph{Beyond binary detection.}
10	Binary detectors~\cite{mitchell2023detectgpt, bao2024fast, hans2024binoculars, basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} achieve strong human-vs.-machine separation but produce no depth estimate.
11	Recent work extends beyond binary labels to collaboration degree~\cite{cheng2025beyond}, contamination hierarchies~\cite{mehta2025beyond}, and multigenerator benchmarks~\cite{wang2024semeval}; robustness concerns arise under paraphrasing~\cite{sadasivan2023detect, krishna2023paraphrasing} and cross-mode transfer~\cite{hao2025rewrite}; CoDeC~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses contamination via membership inference.
12	None estimates recursive generation depth.
13	
14	\paragraph{Data selection and curation.}
15	DSIR~\cite{xie2023dsir}, D4~\cite{tirumala2023d4}, and DoReMi~\cite{xie2023doremi} are depth-agnostic; Drayson et al.~\cite{drayson2025} combined binary detection with importance resampling, a special case when $\Kstar = 1$.
16	
17	\paragraph{Ordinal classification and probing.}
18	Our OBD classifier follows Frank \& Hall's~\cite{frank2001ordinal} binary decomposition; our neural probe extends probing~\cite{alain2017understanding, belinkov2022probing} to the discrimination boundary.
19

1	\section{Consistency Check: $\Kstar$-Guided Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	$\Kempirical = 2$ makes a falsifiable prediction: binary curation should suffice, and graduated performance should converge toward binary as the decay steepness increases.
5	We test this as a consistency check on the boundary measurement, not as an independent contribution.
6	We compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): (i)~\textbf{no-filter}, (ii)~\textbf{binary-ours} ($w = 1 - P(\text{synthetic})$ from 15D LightGBM), (iii)~\textbf{binary-Drayson}~\citep{drayson2025}, (iv)~\textbf{graduated} ($w = \exp(-\alpha \cdot \hat{d})$, $\alpha \in \{0.1,\ldots,0.9\}$), (v)~\textbf{surprisal} (retain above median), and (vi)~\textbf{DSIR}~\citep{xie2023dsir}.
7	We fine-tune Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct via LoRA (rank~8, $\alpha_{\text{LoRA}}{=}16$) for 3 epochs on MMLU and HellaSwag across 5 seeds (51 total runs; this 100\% synthetic setting is an upper bound~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).
8	
9	\begin{table}[t]
10	\centering
11	\small
12	\caption{Downstream task performance (MMLU, HellaSwag) under six data-filtering strategies. Binary-ours uses the 15D LightGBM depth classifier; Graduated applies exponentially decaying weights $w = \exp(-\alpha \hat{d})$. Mean $\pm$ std over 5 seeds. Full $\alpha$ sweep in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.}
13	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
14	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}
15	\end{table}
16

18	These effects are measured in a 100\% synthetic upper-bound setting; real-world contamination ratios are typically lower, and effect sizes would scale accordingly.

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We measure a lower bound on the generational depth discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for prompt-chain recursive synthetic text.
5	For general web text, $\Kempirical \leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale (six architecture families, 1B--8B, $\theta{=}0.60$); across all ten configurations, $\Kempirical \leq 3$, with structured academic text (arXiv) reaching $\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent architectures.
6	This one-wash laundering effect concentrates all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary, and the consistency check confirms that binary curation most effectively minimizes MMLU degradation ($-$0.77\,pp vs.\ $-$1.53\,pp for no-filter).
7	For structured domains where $\Kempirical{=}3$, graduated strategies can exploit one additional depth level, so corpus curators should calibrate filtering to the source domain.
8	The 15 statistical features enable deployment across generators without retraining (80--92\% accuracy retention, $\rho > 0.90$), supporting model-agnostic depth estimation for corpora where the generating model is unknown.
9	Under Bonferroni correction for 10 simultaneous comparisons, three of six $\Kempirical{=}2$ configurations retain significance at the 99.5\% level; the remaining three marginal cases preserve point estimates above $\theta$.
10	$\Kempirical$ converts a continuous AUC decay surface into a discrete operating boundary grounded in the Data Processing Inequality, providing a principled basis for curation decisions that replaces heuristic assumptions about synthetic text recyclability.
11	Several directions remain open: tracking $\Kempirical$ as model scale surpasses 13B, where our scale analysis suggests the boundary may shift toward $\Kempirical{=}1$; bridging the prompt-chain and retrain-chain boundaries, given the substantially higher $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ observed under parameter retraining; and extending depth estimation to additional structured domains and multilingual 
…[truncated 9 chars]

```json
  "old_string": "Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure a lower bound on the generational discrimination boundary, $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable), using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\nUnder prompt-chain generation across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--8B), 3 source domains, and 4 decoding strategies, for web text (C4, Wikipedia), $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ regardless of model family or scale ($\\theta{=}0.60$); across all ten configurations, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$, with structured academic text (arXiv) reaching $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two architectures.\nAmong the six nominal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ configurations, three retain significance under Bonferroni correction; the three marginal cases reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.\nA single generation pass suffices to render general web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \\emph{one-wash laundering} effect.\nModel-free statistical features enable 80--100\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining.\nAs a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text when contamination causes detectable quality loss.",
  "new_string": "Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure a lower bound on the generational discrimination boundary, $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable).\nUnder prompt-chain generation across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--8B), 3 source domains, and 4 decoding strategies, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ for all web text configurations across six architecture families at 1B--8B scale ($\\theta{=}0.60$); structured academic text (arXiv) reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two architectures.\nA single generation pass suffices to render general web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \\emph{one-wash laundering} effect that concentrates all exploitable signal at the human-vs.-machine boundary.\nGenerator-agnostic statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining.\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text."
```

```json
  "old_string": "By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: mutual information with the seed text degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this provides heuristic motivation for expecting $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ to decrease with $k$, though AUC and mutual information are not formally equivalent; the actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.",
  "new_string": "By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: mutual information with the seed text degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this motivates the existence of a finite discrimination boundary where $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ falls below a decision threshold, though AUC and mutual information are not formally equivalent; the actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.\nPairwise AUC may exhibit non-monotonicity at deeper depths due to classifier and feature interactions (e.g., Pythia-1.4B greedy 4v5 $\\auc = 1.0$ vs.\\ 3v4 $= 0.522$; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}), reinforcing that $\\Kempirical$ is a measured quantity rather than a theoretical prediction."
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "Our prompt-chain operates in the replace regime~\\cite{kazdan2025collapse}, making $\\Kempirical = 2$ a conservative lower bound.\nThis literature characterizes macro dynamics but provides no per-text depth estimation, the gap we address.",

}
```

```json
  "old_string": "Our study has three main limitations.\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: we evaluate models up to 8B parameters across 6 architecture families, English text only (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv), and prompt-chain generation; retrain-chain dynamics may yield different boundaries.\n(2)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15 statistical features provide a lower bound on $\\Ktheory$; richer representations (e.g., neural probes) can extend $\\Kempirical$ by one additional depth for some architectures.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold sensitivity}: $\\Kempirical$ varies with the decision threshold $\\theta$, ranging from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; three of ten configurations are marginal at our reference $\\theta{=}0.60$.",
  "new_string": "Our study has four main limitations.\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: we evaluate models up to 8B parameters across 6 architecture families, English text only (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv), and prompt-chain generation; retrain-chain dynamics may yield different boundaries.\nOur model sample is unbalanced: Qwen accounts for 4 of 10 configurations, and we do not evaluate RLHF- or DPO-aligned models, whose generation distributions may differ from the base and instruction-tuned models tested here.\n(2)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15 statistical features provide a lower bound on $\\Ktheory$; richer representations (e.g., neural probes) can extend $\\Kempirical$ by one additional depth for some architectures.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold sensitivity}: $\\Kempirical$ varies with the decision threshold $\\theta$, ranging from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; three of ten configurations are marginal at our reference $\\theta{=}0.60$.\n(4)~\\emph{Domain coverage}: arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is established with only two model families on a single structured domain; broader domain and model coverage is needed to confirm this elevation generalizes."
```

_source line 11533 · 2026-05-23T02:12:34.108798+00:00_

50	Cross-scale validation confirms $\Kempirical = 2$ from 1.5B through 8B: Qwen-7B (1v2 $\auc = 0.6168$ $[0.608, 0.626]$) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (1v2 $\auc = 0.6123$, 0v1 $= 0.9225$) both exceed $\theta$, with progressively lower 0v1 signal consistent with larger models generating more human-like text.
51	Gemma-2-27B (C4 continuation, Qwen cross-scored) yields $\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\auc = 0.5894$), consistent with Gemma-2-2B at 5K ($\Kempirical{=}1$, 1v2 $\auc = 0.5981$). The Gemma family produces $\Kempirical{=}1$ at both 2B and 27B, suggesting $\Kempirical$ is model-family-dependent rather than scale-dependent.
52	
53	The neural probe yields model-dependent $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}}$: $3$ for Qwen (self-familiarity effect from instruction-tuned representations) but $2$ for Pythia; OLMo yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 1$ (1v2 $\auc = 0.5054$), consistent with the 15D LightGBM boundary.
54	The 15D model-free features yield $\Kempirical = 2$ for Pythia and Qwen, while OLMo yields $\Kempirical = 1$ under the standardized pipeline.
55	
56	\subsection{LLM Zero-Shot Baseline}
57	\label{sec:extended_llm}
58	
59	GPT-5.5 achieves 33.2\% accuracy (MAE = 1.10) and Claude Opus~4.6 33.1\% (MAE = 1.15) on a balanced 500-sample test set, comparable to OBD-LightGBM's 38.0\% (95\% CI: $[0.340, 0.422]$; McNemar $p = 0.081$, $0.069$; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).

38	\begin{figure}[t]
39	\centering
40	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_auc_decay.pdf}
41	\caption{Pairwise $\auc$ decay across depth pairs for model-mode configurations (solid) and decoding strategies (dashed); shaded bands show bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals. The horizontal dashed line marks $\theta = 0.60$.}
42	\label{fig:auc_decay}
43	\end{figure}
44	
45	Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay} show $\Kempirical \leq 2$ across all 10 model configurations: 6/10 configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Qwen with 4 configs, LLaMA-8B, and Pythia-1.4B, spanning three of six families) (1v2 $\auc$ range: 0.6074--0.6955; five of six CI lower bounds $> \theta$; permutation $p < 0.001$), while OLMo-1B, Mistral-7B, Pythia-6.9B, and Gemma-2-2B yield $\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\auc$: 0.500, 0.575, 0.596, and 0.598, all below $\theta$; Section~\ref{sec:boundary_cases}).
46	Under Bonferroni correction for 10 simultaneous comparisons (99.5\% CIs), three of six boundary configurations retain CI lower bounds above $\theta$; the three most marginal cases, Pythia-1.4B (0.593), Qwen-continuation (0.598), and LLaMA-8B (0.599), fall below the widened interval.
47	All three retain point estimates above $\theta$; Qwen-continuation and LLaMA-8B also retain uncorrected CI lower bounds above $\theta$, while Pythia-1.4B (0.598) falls marginally below. The near-misses reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.

156	%% ====================================================================
157	\subsection{Binary Detector Confidence}
158	\label{sec:binary_confidence}
159	
160	Binary detectors (DivEye~\cite{basani2026diveye}, Binoculars~\cite{hans2024binoculars}) show moderate rank correlations with depth ($|\rho| = 0.06$--$0.46$; Table~\ref{tab:s9_binary_spearman}) but fail as depth proxies: 6-class accuracy is at or below chance ($\leq 0.199$ vs.\ 0.167 baseline), pairwise $\auc$ yields $\Kempirical^{\text{binary}} \leq 1$, and multiple conditions exhibit non-monotonic AUC at deeper pairs.
161	The observed correlation is an artifact of 0v1 separation rather than fine-grained depth signal.
162
